# M1 technical plan: the team on your login

How M1 (issues #10 to #21 and #24) is built. Written from a map of OpenBot's remote-agent protocol made on 2026-11-08; file references are to `openbot/`. Read with [ARCHITECTURE.md](ARCHITECTURE.md) §3 and [PROVIDERS.md](PROVIDERS.md).

## 1. Shape

```
OpenBot server ──AG-UI POST /──────────────► agent-freeduet (Python, port 4230, one service for all ten members of v0.1)
   ▲   (tools in RunAgentInput.tools,              │  reads the member's prompt from agents.yaml by forwardedProps.openbotBotId
   │    member id in forwardedProps)               │  runs: claude -p (official CLI, the person's own login, no built-in tools)
   │                                               │        └─ MCP server "freeduet" (stdio) = the tools OpenBot offered
   │◄── TOOL_CALL_* + RUN_FINISHED ────────────────┘  a tool call ends the segment; OpenBot runs it (grants, policy, approval gate, audit)
   └── next POST with the tool result ─────────────►  a fresh, stateless claude -p continues from the full transcript

OpenBot server ──Anthropic POST /v1/messages─► agent-freeduet: the server's own short model calls (titles, routing, tool selection,
                 (ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL)              auto-review, triage, voice summary), text only, routed by purpose
```

## 2. Decisions

| # | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| D1 | Members are `remote-ag-ui` agents whose endpoint is exactly `MANAGED_AGENT_AG_UI_URL` (`http://localhost:4230/`) | Only that URL gets `x-openbot-agent-token` (`server/src/agents/managed-endpoint.ts`); one endpoint may serve many agent ids |
| D2 | `agents.yaml`: `type: ${FREEDUET_MEMBER_TYPE:-remote-ag-ui}`, `endpoint: ${MANAGED_AGENT_AG_UI_URL:-}`, and `system_prompt` kept | `FREEDUET_ROUTER=off` is `FREEDUET_MEMBER_TYPE=built-in` (precedent: `examples/fintech/agents.yaml`). The prompt stays in one file; agent-freeduet reads it by id |
| D3 | The member is identified by `forwardedProps.openbotBotId` alone, and a run without it is refused. This Bot's standing role (`standing-role:<id>`) is accepted wherever it is in `messages`; its prompt is the `agents.yaml` row, else that role. A standing role or holdings row naming another Bot (`standing-role:<other>`, `granted-tools:<other>`) never joins the system prompt: it is quoted as context at the top of the conversation | Neither URL nor body carries an agent id. The bot id is set by OpenBot's own middleware (`copilot.ts`, runWith) and reaches agent-freeduet only over the token-checked managed endpoint. The role is not always first: on a hop, middleware inside the one that adds it puts `openbot:personal-memory` and `openduet:channel` in front, and since #33 a browser chat's clone carries them too (`toolkit/src/copilotkit/agents.ts`); each is said once. The old `messages[0]` check refused every hop (fixtures `agent-freeduet/tests/fixtures/handoff_*_run.json`, recorded by `server/tests/freeduet-handoff.test.ts`) |
| D4 | **Tools stay in-band.** OpenBot's offered tools (`RunAgentInput.tools`) become the MCP server's tools. When the CLI calls one, agent-freeduet emits `TOOL_CALL_START/ARGS/END`, then `RUN_FINISHED`, and stops the CLI. OpenBot executes the call with its own approval gate and POSTs again with the result | This is the only path where approvals can pause and resume (`toolkit/src/headless-tools.ts`). The callback route can never wait for an approval and refuses instead (`server/src/approvals/types.ts:308-317`) |
| D5 | Only names in `forwardedProps.openbotDeploymentTools` go to `POST /api/agent-tools/call` with `AGENT_TOOL_TOKEN` and `forwardedProps.openbotRun`; their result goes straight back to the CLI. For FreeDuet's own members that list is `ask_person` only: the server runs their `message_bot` in-band like any other tool (`server/src/freeduet/coordination.ts`). If the callback refuses a hand-off only because the person has to be asked, agent-freeduet ends the segment and asks the person for that hand-off (who and what) in the conversation | That route exists for them (`server/src/app.ts:1842`), but it can never ask the person: an `ask` from the approval gate there is refused, and with auto-review on (OpenBot's default) the reviewer is shown no request at all, because the route carries no conversation. Iris's hand-off to Penny was refused that way. In-band, the gate reads the conversation and a pending approval suspends the run (D4) |
| D6 | **Stateless segments.** Every POST starts a new `claude -p --no-session-persistence` with the member prompt and the whole conversation rendered from `messages` (system rows, user, assistant, tool calls and results). No CLI session is resumed | Approval resumes arrive as a new run after the CLI is gone, restarts lose nothing, and segments are uniform. Costs more tokens per turn; optimise in M3 with continuity checkpoints |
| D7 | CLI lockdown, verified against the installed CLI's `--help`: `-p --output-format stream-json --verbose --include-partial-messages --no-session-persistence --tools "" --strict-mcp-config --mcp-config <freeduet only> --disable-slash-commands`, MCP tools pre-allowed (`--allowedTools mcp__freeduet`), `--append-system-prompt` for the member prompt and OpenBot's system rows; the conversation on stdin | No file, shell or web access except through OpenBot's gateway; no other MCP server; no permission prompts in a headless CLI (approvals are OpenBot's) |
| D8 | An SSE comment (`: keepalive`) every 15 s while the CLI works | OpenBot ends a turn after 60 s of silence (`AGENT_STALL_TIMEOUT_MS`) |
| D9 | The CLI's sign-in lives in a named volume mounted as the CLI's config directory. The person signs in once with `docker compose exec -it agent-freeduet claude`. **No FreeDuet code reads that directory** (test M1.8) | PROVIDERS.md rule 1 |
| D10 | Server-side model calls: `BOT_PROVIDER=anthropic`, `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL=http://localhost:4230`, `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY=$FREEDUET_LOCAL_KEY` (a random key start.sh writes; agent-freeduet requires it in `x-api-key`). agent-freeduet's `POST /v1/messages` answers text-only requests and routes by purpose: auto-review (`approvals/policy.ts`) on a tier A engine; titles, routing, tool selection, triage and voice summaries on the free assistants chain, falling back to Claude Code. Purpose is recognised from each caller's fixed prompt markers, with a test per marker | Zero changes to the server's call sites; the server needs no real API key (docs/SERVER_MODEL_CALLS.md) |
| D11 | Engines: `engines.yaml` loaded and validated; the chain is always ordered subscription, then free, then metered (except a member in own_order: D115, section 74); metered never runs while `metered.enabled` is false or a cap is 0. M1 implements the `claude-code` engine and the `openai-compatible` engine for free assistants (Ollama); the others are declared and refuse clearly | M1.7 |
| D12 | Not signed in, or a subscription limit: `RUN_ERROR` with code `OPENBOT_MODEL_AUTH_REQUIRED` for sign-in, and a plain message for limits | OpenBot records the first as a sign-in failure |
| D13 | Work reports and checkpoints (M1.13): after each segment agent-freeduet saves a checkpoint with the maintainer's own checkpoint writer (MIT, attributed) under `/data/handoff/<botId>/<threadId>/` in its own volume | The member computers are other containers; one place to read on an engine switch |

## 3. Interface contract (fixed, so work can run in parallel)

| Item | Value |
|---|---|
| Package | `openbot/agent-freeduet/`, Python 3.12, FastAPI + uvicorn, deps pinned in `requirements.txt`, tests in `tests/` with pytest |
| Port | 4230, published on `127.0.0.1` and `[::1]` only |
| Routes | `GET /health`; `POST /` (AG-UI, SSE); `POST /v1/messages` (Anthropic format, text only, non-streaming) |
| Auth | `x-openbot-agent-token == MANAGED_AGENT_TOKEN` on `POST /`; `x-api-key == FREEDUET_LOCAL_KEY` on `/v1/messages`; `hmac.compare_digest` |
| Env | `MANAGED_AGENT_TOKEN`, `AGENT_TOOL_TOKEN`, `OPENBOT_TOOL_URL` (default `http://host.docker.internal:3001/api/agent-tools/call`), `FREEDUET_LOCAL_KEY`, `FREEDUET_AGENTS_FILE` (default `/app/tenant/agents.yaml`), `FREEDUET_ENGINES_FILE` (default `/app/engines.yaml`, falling back to `engines.example.yaml`), `FREEDUET_DATA_DIR` (default `/data`), `CLAUDE_BIN` (default `claude`) |
| Modules | `freeduet/app.py` (routes), `freeduet/agui.py` (RunAgentInput parsing, event encoding, keepalive), `freeduet/members.py` (agents.yaml by id), `freeduet/transcript.py` (messages to prompt), `freeduet/claude_cli.py` (command, process, stream-json parsing), `freeduet/bridge/` (stdio MCP server + loopback IPC with the parent), `freeduet/engines.py` (load, validate, order, caps), `freeduet/server_calls.py` (`/v1/messages`, purpose routing; not `aux.py`, a reserved file name on Windows), `freeduet/assistants.py` (OpenAI-compatible free engine), `freeduet/checkpoints.py` (port of the maintainer's checkpoint writer) |
| Ported code | From Anil-matcha/open-duet (Apache-2.0) and the maintainer's own checkpoint writer (MIT): keep the license header, name the original file, say "Modified for FreeDuet" |
| Tests that must exist | fake `claude` executable that replays stream-json fixtures and calls MCP tools; a full segment loop (text, tool call, `RUN_FINISHED`, resume with result); deployment-tool callback; keepalive; auth refusals; logins-first ordering (metered never first, never with caps 0); credential directory never opened (M1.8); purpose routing per marker; checkpoint written per segment |

| Stream | Issues | Owns |
|---|---|---|
| A. Member runtime | #10, #11, #12, #13 | `app.py`, `agui.py`, `members.py`, `transcript.py`, `claude_cli.py`, `bridge/`, Dockerfile |
| B. Engines and server calls | #15, #16, #17 | `engines.py`, `server_calls.py`, `assistants.py`, credential-directory test |
| C. Wiring | #14 | `docker-compose.yml` service, `scripts/start.sh` (start, health-wait, `FREEDUET_LOCAL_KEY`), `.env.example`, `agents.yaml` interpolation, `server/tests/freeduet-package.test.ts`, README quick start, `docs/UPSTREAM_TOUCHES.md` |
| D. Evals | #18 | `evals/team/run.ts` (or Python) and its fake-engine mode |
| E. Checkpoints | #24 | `checkpoints.py` |
| Maintainer | #19, #20, #21 | Real-login eval run, Google Workspace grants, clean-machine start |

## 5. Team memory, handoff and oversight (M1.14 to M1.16)

Added on 2026-11-08 at the maintainer's request: members must remember, pass information to each other, and Iris must know every success and failure and correct what needs correcting. Checked against the code first:

| Need | Already in OpenBot | Missing |
|---|---|---|
| Memory of the person | `server/src/memory/` personal memories; a memory a Bot formed is delivered only to that Bot (`db/schema/memory.ts`) | Nothing for M1 |
| Memory of the work | Checkpoints (M1.13, D13) | Nobody reads them back until M3 |
| Memory of the team (what one member learned that others need) | None | Lessons (D16) |
| Passing work and information | `message_bot` with a typed envelope (task, constraints, expecting) and the answer relayed back to the asker, failures included (`agents/handoff*.ts`); caps `BOT_HANDOFF_MAX_DEPTH=1`, `BOT_HANDOFF_MAX_PER_RUN=3` | No Bot-to-Bot grant exists for FreeDuet: `mayAddress` reads `plugin_grants` of kind `bot` (`index.ts:1267`) and the package seeds only skill grants, so today Iris can address nobody |
| Iris knows successes and failures | Report lines and Iris's rules (F24) | Iris sees a report only when she asked for the work. A member that fails in its own conversation with the person is invisible to her |
| Correctives that last | None | Lessons (D16) and a daily review (D17) |

| # | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| D14 | **Hub grants.** A FreeDuet module seeds `plugin_grants` of kind `bot` after package sync: Iris to each of the nine members, each member to Iris, nothing member to member. Marked `granted_by = freeduet-package`, idempotent, removing only its own rows. Depth stays 1 | Iris hands out work and gets the answer back by relay; a member the person talks to directly can report to Iris; no chains. An admin can still add or revoke grants by hand |
| D15 | **Team log.** After each segment agent-freeduet appends one line to `/data/team-log.jsonl`: time, member, thread, outcome, one-line summary, whether Iris asked for it, approval ids from the "Already" line. Iris gets one read-only tool `team_log(since, member?, outcome?)` from agent-freeduet's own MCP server, next to OpenBot's tools. Shared rule: when Iris did not ask for the work and the outcome is failed, blocked or needs you, the member also sends its report to Iris with `message_bot` | Iris sees every outcome, also of work she did not hand out. The tool reads FreeDuet's own data only, never the person's apps |
| D16 | **Lessons.** Iris may propose a lesson for a member ("Penny: check the VAT number before drafting an invoice"), with the evidence. A lesson is stored `pending` in `/data/lessons/<member>.json` and becomes `active` only after the person approves it in chat; a Bot's message never approves one. agent-freeduet adds that member's active lessons to its prompt on every run. The person can list, change and remove lessons by asking Iris. A failure with the same cause twice in seven days makes Iris ask Leo for a proposal to change the member's prompt or skills; applying it stays with the person | Corrections that last, never silent: a permanent change in behaviour is the person's decision |
| D17 | **Daily review.** A routine for Iris, every morning (packages cannot declare routines, so the FreeDuet module of #25 created it once per person, through `routines/store.ts`, and the person can change or stop it; since D79, section 58, it is only offered and is made after the person says yes and allows the card): read `team_log` for the last 24 hours, give the person one summary (done, failed with cause, pending lessons to approve, what she already corrected) | One place to see the team's day |
| D18 | **Report guard.** When a segment ends a turn that did work (a tool call in the turn, an attached file, or a message that is neither just a greeting nor ends with a question: the shared no-report rule) and its text has no report, agent-freeduet runs one more `claude -p` with no tools at all, the conversation so far and a fixed instruction to write only the six lines from what the conversation shows; a complete report is streamed as the end of the same message, anything else adds nothing. Once per turn; the team log's `report_guard` says it asked (`agent-freeduet/freeduet/report_guard.py`). A turn never ends with no text for the person: when the final message is empty and the guard adds nothing (or the turn did no work, so it never asked), the person gets one fixed sentence, "I stopped without an answer. The last thing I tried was <tool name>. Tell me to go on, or tell me what you want me to do instead.", with the name of the last tool called and no page, result or argument; the CLI's own result text stands in for the answer when no delta carried it | Live runs (#19) still ended two or three work turns a run without a report (iris-3, otto-1, vera-5), and a missing report hides that work from Iris and the checkpoint. A live run of jules-3 ended a booking turn with no text at all after a failed click: the app's handler had thrown, the SDK ran no follow-up (fixed in the app and the runner, `docs/UPSTREAM_TOUCHES.md`), and agent-freeduet had no answer of its own for an engine that says nothing |
| D19 | **Exact reasons for a missing tool.** When a member's prompt relies on `message_bot` and OpenBot did not offer it, agent-freeduet adds the exact reason it can see to the system text: a relay or a hop runs at OpenBot's hand-off depth limit (`BOT_HANDOFF_MAX_DEPTH=1`), so the next step goes out on the person's next message; otherwise OpenBot gave no reason. Every member is told to give the reason a refused tool result gives, never "unavailable" alone (`tool_notes.py`, `transcript.RUNTIME_NOTE`) | iris-2: in the relay of Jules's answer (depth 1, no `message_bot`) Iris told the person the messaging tool was unavailable and wrote a brief to paste |
| D21 | **Hand-offs along the hub are pre-approved.** Decided by the maintainer on 2026-11-09 (#42). A `message_bot` call (`bot/message_bot`, effect `delegate`) never asks the person when the loaded package is FreeDuet's and `plugin_grants` holds a row of kind `bot` for exactly that pair (the asking Bot to the Bot it names, resolved as the desk resolves it) with `granted_by = freeduet-package` (D14): Iris to a member, and a member's report to Iris. It is read per call from the table `mayAddress` reads, never from a list of names. `server/src/freeduet/hub-handoffs.ts` wraps the gate the coordination tools are given (`agents/coordination-wiring.ts`); every other call reaches the gate unchanged. Each pre-approved hand-off writes one `approval.evaluated` row (`source: freeduet_hub`, with the run id) beside the desk's own `agent.handoff_offered`, and the hop is queued and shown as before. Limits: a grant an admin made by hand (even for the same pair), a Bot with no hub grant (the desk refuses it as before), another package, the depth cap (`BOT_HANDOFF_MAX_DEPTH=1`), the fan-out cap, the pause check and the private-share check are as upstream. The member that receives the work is gated on everything it does, with the safety floor, the rules and the reviewer, so a task that ends in a send or a payment still asks the person at that action. A hand-off the person once declined is no longer refused for that reason, and a request opened before this change is still re-checked by the gate itself | A fresh eval stack (no standing "always allow", `FREEDUET_INTELLIGENCE=off`): one night of team evals made 46 approval requests, all for `message_bot`: every member's status report to Iris (concierge 10, writer 6, bookkeeper 6, counsel 6, creator 4, reviewer 3, fixer 2) and some of Iris's hand-offs (Jules 3, Portia 3, Vera 1, Leo 1), each ending its run in an interrupt. Auto-review is on by default and reads every action that is not a read, and a reviewer shown no request that names the hand-off says `needs_approval` (`approvals/policy.ts`, `decideAction`). On the maintainer's own deployment it did not happen only because he had already chosen "always allow". The team talking to itself is not an account action |
| D22 | **A relay turn gets the member's computer; a forward hop does not.** Decided by the maintainer on 2026-11-09 (#61, options A to E). The answer to a member's hand-off (Iris's reply to Jules's report) comes back to the member as a RELAY, a turn in the person's own conversation (`agents/handoff-runner.ts`, `relay`, `answerIn`). It now gets the same computer tools the member would get for an ordinary server-run turn of that Bot, built by the same `createHeadlessComputerTools` that routines, wake-ups and group turns use, behind the same `canUseComputer` check, and run under the same `HeadlessToolsMiddleware` (`agents/handoff-delivery.ts`, `toolsForTurn`; `freeduet/relay-computer.ts`). Nothing is added: no computer in the deployment, or a Bot that is not the person's to drive, gives none, and every call still goes through the computer gateway's policy and the approval gate, so a click, a typed word, a command or a purchase step opens an approval request in the person's conversation and nothing reaches the computer before they decide. D21's pre-approval covers `bot/message_bot` and never a computer or an account action. The FORWARD hop (Iris hands Jules the work, which runs in a scratch thread nobody sees) keeps `tools: []`, and D19's sibling note (`tool_notes.py`, #61) still tells the member why. Limits: the forward hop stays without a computer, so work handed to a member that needs a browser is not researched there; a relay turn is still a hand-off turn (depth 1, no `message_bot`, D14); a deployment whose capability switch turns the browser off offers the tools and the gateway refuses each call, as for a routine. When the person switches the computer off, no server-run turn is offered it: the capability switches the app already filters on (`cloudBrowser`, `cloudNetwork`, `cloudComputer`, read through `capabilitiesFor`) hide the matching computer tools in every relay, routine, wake-up, group turn and approval resume, and the gateway's refusal stays as the second line of defence. | Found in the team evals of 8 to 9 November 2026 (#61): Jules reports to Iris with `message_bot`, Iris's answer comes back as a relay, and a Jules that went on researching there called a tool the CLI did not list and told the person its browser had broken. Hand-off delivery passed `tools: []` to every hop. The relay runs where the person is, so an approval it raises is seen and answered there; the forward hop's scratch thread would hide it until the delivery deadline. |

Tests that must exist: a seeded grant lets Iris address Penny and refuses Penny to Otto; the team log gets one line per segment with the right outcome; `team_log` is offered to Iris only; a lesson stays pending without the person's approval and a Bot's message does not activate it; an active lesson appears in the member's prompt; each person gets the daily review routine once, and not again after they delete it. Hand-offs (`server/tests/freeduet-handoff.test.ts`, `agent-freeduet/tests/test_handoffs.py`): a hop to a member and the relay back to Iris replay OpenBot's recorded payloads and run; the member's answer reaches Iris's conversation; Iris's `message_bot` runs in-band, the gate reads what the person asked, and an `ask` waits for the person and sends the hand-off once on approval; a callback refusal that needs the person ends the turn with a question to them. D21 (`server/tests/freeduet-handoff-preapproved.test.ts`, real Postgres, through `wireCoordination`): on a fresh database Iris to Portia and Gus's report to Iris open no approval request and still queue and audit the hop; a Bot with no hub grant is refused as before; an admin-made grant, a package that is not FreeDuet's and a name two Bots share keep the gate; and a hand-off whose task ends in an email or a payment still asks at the receiving member's own action.

## 6. Conversations and memory without CopilotKit Intelligence (#42)

Added on 2026-11-08 at the maintainer's request. Before #42, OpenBot refused to start without the three `INTELLIGENCE_*` settings (`openbot/server/src/config.ts`), so every FreeDuet install needed a CopilotKit account.

| # | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| D20 | **CopilotKit Intelligence optional, off by default.** `FREEDUET_INTELLIGENCE=off` or `on`. Unset was upstream OpenBot, unchanged (replaced by D65: refused). With `off` the server boots with no `INTELLIGENCE_*` key, keeps conversations, history and memory in FreeDuet's own Postgres, and sends nothing to CopilotKit. Telemetry is off unless `COPILOTKIT_TELEMETRY_DISABLED=false` is set. With `on` the server needs the three keys, as upstream. **Since 17 November 2026 (D65, section 45): a missing `FREEDUET_INTELLIGENCE` is refused at start-up, no longer read as upstream's `on`; the desktop app writes `off` when its settings hold no value (D67, section 47)** | FreeDuet promises self-hosted, zero cost and no API key. Intelligence keeps its own copy of every thread, user memory, browser trajectory and product analytics. Its free hosted tier (1,000 interactions per organization per month) would stop a real user quickly. CopilotKit's docs say the TypeScript runtime works without Intelligence; OpenBot made it mandatory |

How the default is chosen: `scripts/start.sh` writes `off` into a new `.env`. An existing `.env` with all three Intelligence settings and no `FREEDUET_INTELLIGENCE` line gets `on`, with a notice printed, so an install from before #42 keeps its conversations in Intelligence.

Measured in #45: with `off` and no telemetry variable, a boot, a chat turn, a headless turn, a hop and memory create and recall sent zero requests to any `*.copilotkit.ai` host. With `COPILOTKIT_TELEMETRY_DISABLED=false` the same probe does see telemetry, so the probe can see requests at all.

FreeDuet's own migrations live in `openbot/server/src/freeduet/threads/migrations/`, with their own journal and bookkeeping table, `freeduet_thread_migrations`. They are recorded by name and hash, run in one transaction behind an advisory lock, and are safe when two processes boot at once. An applied migration whose file has changed is refused.

| Table | Holds |
|---|---|
| `freeduet_threads` | One row per conversation: thread id, user, agent, name, times |
| `freeduet_thread_runs` | One row per run: run id, parent run, status (`running`, `finished`, `stopped`, `failed`, `interrupted`), the run's events compacted as `jsonb`, what the run was asked, and the runner that wrote it |
| `freeduet_thread_locks` | One row per thread while a run holds it: run id, user, agent, expiry. An expired lock is taken over |

Messages are not stored in a table of their own. They are rebuilt from the runs' events with AG-UI's own reducer.

Limits with `off`:

- Conversations already in Intelligence are not imported. An importer is a possible later phase.
- Switching from `off` to `on` starts fresh. Neither side copies the other's conversations.
- Memory is local. Recall is a text match, not a semantic search.
- Learned skills are off.
- Live tail (following a run that is still going, after a reload or from another tab) works in one server process only. FreeDuet supports one replica.
- A server restart in the middle of a run marks that run `interrupted` and keeps the text it had written.
- Keys and Learning variables left in `.env` are ignored, and the boot log names them once.

## 7. Gmail and Calendar with an app password (D24)

Added on 2026-11-10. The OAuth way to connect Gmail and Google Calendar (`google-workspace`) needs a Google Cloud project, an OAuth client, a public address for the consent screen to return to, and, for a person outside a Workspace domain, either a 7-day expiry (an app in testing) or Google's security assessment (CASA) for the Gmail scopes. That is a lot to ask of one person with one mailbox, on a project that promises a simple self-hosted install.

| # | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| D24 | **A second, simpler way to connect Gmail and Calendar, next to the OAuth one.** A new catalogue entry, "Gmail and Google Calendar (app password)", key `google-imap`, auth kind `user-secret`, transport `google-imap`. The person pastes three things on their own connection page: their Gmail address, a Google app password (`myaccount.google.com/apppasswords`) and, optionally, the calendar's secret address in iCal format. Mail goes over IMAP and SMTP, the calendar over the iCal address. The OAuth entry is unchanged and both can be connected at once. **The same five tools** as the OAuth entry, with the same names, arguments and JSON answers and the same read and write lists (`gmail_search_messages`, `gmail_read_message` and `calendar_list_events` read; `gmail_send_reply` and, since the amendment below, `gmail_send_message` write, so each waits for the person's approval, apart from the one case D27 names); a test runs both adapters on the same fixtures and compares the answers. **Hosts are pinned in code** (`mail-presets.ts`): `imap.gmail.com:993` and `smtp.gmail.com:465`, both TLS, and a calendar address only from `https://calendar.google.com/calendar/ical/`. Any other host, a non-https address or a redirect off that host is refused; the catalogue row's address is never dialled. **Read-only mail**: the mailbox is opened with `EXAMINE`, bodies are fetched with `BODY.PEEK`, there is no tool that moves, deletes, labels or flags, and a test drives the real `imapflow` against a fake IMAP server and checks every command on the wire. Search is Gmail's own (`X-GM-RAW`, default `in:inbox newer_than:1d`), ids are the hex of `X-GM-MSGID` and `X-GM-THRID` (the ids the REST API uses), and the caps and timeouts are the REST adapter's. **A reply stays in its thread**: `In-Reply-To` and `References` come from the original, the subject is the one the Bot gives, with "Re:" added when it is missing, and the recipients are limited to people already on it; no new recipient can be added. A new recipient is only reachable through `gmail_send_message` (amendment below), which is a different tool with its own approval. **All three values are secrets**, stored together as one encrypted value of a new credential kind, `mcp_user_secret` (migration 0068), per person. They are never logged, never in an audit row, never in an error sentence and never returned: the status shows the address and whether a calendar is attached. Saving checks them first (one IMAP login, and one fetch of the calendar if an address was given) and stores nothing unless they work; a wrong password gets a fixed sentence that repeats nothing that was typed. Disconnecting revokes the stored value here at once, and removing the connector or the person revokes it as well. Presets are a table (`mail-presets.ts`) so another provider could be added later; none is. **The catalogue key is `google-imap` and must not spell "password":** the approval policy's credentials rule (`approvals/policy.ts`, `SAFETY_REQUIREMENTS`) matches the word in a tool's reference and hands the action to the person with "changing a password is always done by the person", so under a key containing it every write, `gmail_send_reply` included, could never be approved. A test runs the real gate and holds this | Gmail is where most people's mail and calendar are, and an app password is the only way to reach it without a Google Cloud project. IMAP and SMTP are standard and Gmail's extensions (`X-GM-RAW`, `X-GM-MSGID`, `X-GM-THRID`) give the same search, ids and threads the REST adapter returns, so the Bots, the morning brief and the setup checklist work on either connection without being taught a second shape. The brief asks the OAuth connection first and the app-password one only when the first is refused. Libraries are `imapflow` (MIT), `nodemailer` (MIT-0) and `node-ical` (Apache-2.0), listed in `NOTICE.md`; nothing copyleft. The wire test exists because a fake that implements only what the transport calls cannot show what the library actually sends |

Limits:

- Gmail only in v0.1. Another provider is a new row in `mail-presets.ts` and a review of its extensions, not a setting.
- The person needs 2-Step Verification on their Google account, or Google offers no app passwords. A Workspace administrator can switch app passwords off for the whole domain, and then this way is unavailable to those people.
- **An app password is wider than any OAuth scope.** At Google it opens the whole mailbox: read, move, delete and send. Nothing Google does narrows it, so the tools (the five above, no others) and the approval on every send (but the one case D27 names) are the whole boundary. A person who wants Google to enforce a narrower grant should use the OAuth entry.
- Disconnecting here forgets the password at once, but this deployment cannot revoke it at Google. Only the person can, at `myaccount.google.com/apppasswords`, and the connection page says so.
- A reply returns no message id (SMTP gives none; the answer carries an empty `id`). Category labels in a search are rebuilt with extra searches and are left out of a single message read.
- The calendar is read-only and comes from the iCal address, which Google refreshes slowly, so a just-made event may take a while to show. Answers are cached for about 60 seconds per person. A person who resets the address at Google must paste the new one.
- The iCal address is a credential: anyone holding it reads the calendar. It is kept like the password.
- The morning brief asks the OAuth connection first. A person with both connected and an expired OAuth token gets the OAuth error rather than the app-password answer, because only a refusal, not a vendor's error, moves it on.
- `approvals/policy.ts` was not changed. If the maintainer prefers a key that says "app password", the rule needs an exemption for this key first.

### D24 amendment, 2026-11-12: a fifth tool, `gmail_send_message`

Iris refused "send an email saying good morning" because Otto could only answer a message (`gmail_send_reply`, inside an existing thread, to people already on it). The maintainer decided that a member must be able to send a **new** email. One tool is added to **both** adapters, with the same name, arguments and JSON answer, so the parity rule above now covers five tools.

- **Arguments.** `to` (one to five plain addresses, no display name), `subject` (one line, at most 200 characters) and `body` (plain text, at most 20,000 characters). Nothing else: a Cc, a Bcc, an attachment, HTML, a custom header, a "from" or a thread id that is filled in is refused, not ignored, so what the person is shown is what is sent. One function checks them for both adapters (`parseNewMessage`, `google-workspace-rest.ts`), with an address test deliberately narrower than RFC 5322 (`mail-address.ts`): ASCII only, no quoted part, no comment, no literal, no control character, which also refuses look-alike letters and a header injected through a line break. Every refusal is a fixed sentence that repeats nothing it was given and ends "It was not sent."; an address or a body may have come from a page the Bot read, and an error is read back into its model.
- **OAuth adapter.** One POST to `/gmail/v1/users/me/messages/send` with the message as `raw`, no `threadId` and no look-up first. The scope `gmail.send` already allows sending a new message, so **no scope was added** and the consent screen and every saved connection are unchanged.
- **App-password adapter.** One SMTP send through the same pinned `smtp.gmail.com:465` preset and `nodemailer` path as the reply, from the connected address, with the envelope set to exactly the validated recipients, no Cc, no `In-Reply-To` and no `References`. **The mailbox is not opened at all**: no IMAP connection is made, so there is no `APPEND` and nothing is filed by this tool (Gmail files the sent copy itself). The answer is `{"sent":true,"id":"","threadId":""}`, the reply's shape; the OAuth adapter fills the ids Gmail gives. A recipient Gmail refuses is reported with "The email went to the others."
- **A write in the catalogue for both entries.** `writeTools` is `["gmail_send_reply", "gmail_send_message"]` on `google-workspace` and on `google-imap`; a recorded "read" cannot narrow it, as for the reply. Background runs (a check-in, research) are never offered it, because they are limited to reads.
- **Asked about every time.** Unlike a reply, "Always allow" is neither offered nor honoured for a new email: it is opened as a request with the policy `always_ask`, the approval store refuses `allow_always` for it, a saved rule for the same address does not answer it, and the app hides the button. A standing answer for one address must never send a later, different email. The card shows every recipient, the subject and the whole body (folded behind "Show all N characters" when long, never cut); the chat-channel card is bounded and says so ("Not all shown").
- **Who has it.** Only Otto, through the new skill `send-a-new-email` (`examples/freeduet/skills.yaml`); the grant of the tool is an administrator's, as for every connector tool, and is not seeded. Iris, Penny and every other member are not offered it. Otto's prompt says a reply goes through `gmail_send_reply` and a new email through `gmail_send_message`, that an address is used exactly as written (never invented, corrected or completed, and asked about when it looks wrong), and that after sending he says plainly that it was sent, to whom, its subject and the text. Iris hands the request to Otto instead of refusing.
- **When it needs no card at all**, see D27 below.

Limits of the amendment: the send is one message per call and one call per exact email per run; no send-later, no draft, no template. A message sent over SMTP returns no id.

## 8. Dictation with a local Whisper, off by default (D25)

Added on 2026-11-11, after the maintainer said yes to the microphone. Dictation already existed (an OpenAI-compatible transcription adapter, `server/src/dictation/`), but it needed a service and, for most people, a key. This adds a Whisper that runs beside the stack, so a person who wants dictation can have it with no key, no account and no recording leaving the computer.

| # | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| D25 | **A local Whisper, optional, off by default, switched on by one line in `.env`: `FREEDUET_DICTATION=local`.** (1) **A `whisper` service** in `docker-compose.yml`, behind the profile `whisper` so nothing starts or builds without the flag. It is built from `openbot/whisper/Dockerfile`: whisper.cpp **`v1.9.5`, commit `d1be6fde11ac6e0407606b4e42fe72d34add8037`** (checked with `git rev-parse HEAD` on the tag), built for the CPU with ffmpeg beside it, running as an unprivileged user (uid 10002) on a read-only root with every capability dropped, and published on **`127.0.0.1:${WHISPER_PORT:-4240}` only**. Its `whisper-server` serves the OpenAI path (`--inference-path /v1/audio/transcriptions`), so the **existing adapter is used unchanged**: `TRANSCRIPTION_BASE_URL=http://127.0.0.1:4240/v1`. (2) **The model** is `FREEDUET_WHISPER_MODEL`, from a closed list (`large-v3-turbo-q5_0` by default, `large-v3-turbo`, `small`, `base`). It is downloaded on first start into the named volume `whisper-models`, over https, from `huggingface.co/ggerganov/whisper.cpp`, and **checked against a SHA-256 and a size written in the repository** (`shared/whisper-models.ts`, which says where each was read and how to check it by hand). A file that does not match is deleted and the service stops with one sentence; nothing unchecked is loaded. (3) **`start.sh` with the flag** adds the service to the ones it starts and, only when `TRANSCRIPTION_PROVIDER`, `TRANSCRIPTION_BASE_URL` and `TRANSCRIPTION_MODEL` are all unset, writes them once, pointing at it, and prints one line (local, and the size of the first download). Any one of the three set by the person means nothing is written. **With the flag unset nothing changes**; a test holds the exact `docker compose up` line. (4) **Two optional settings**, for every provider and not only the local one: `TRANSCRIPTION_LANGUAGE` (two letters from Whisper's own list, sent as `language`) and `TRANSCRIPTION_PROMPT` (500 characters at most, sent as `prompt`). Unset, the request is byte for byte the old one, and a test holds it against a literal recorded from the unchanged adapter. The local service is told the language (see the second amendment). (5) **Silence must not become invented text.** The server runs with `--suppress-nst`, a no-speech threshold of 0.6 and, as first written, voice-activity detection off (on since the second amendment below), all from `examples/server/server.cpp` and `src/whisper.cpp` at the pinned tag; and a transcript that is empty or only whitespace now ends as "No speech was detected" for **every** provider (the route tested `!text`, so `"\n"` passed as a sentence). There is **no list of banned sentences**: it is added only if proof 7c fails on the maintainer's computer, with the sentence it caught. (6) **The segments' line breaks are joined** (`joinSegments`, set with the flag): whisper.cpp ends every segment with `\n`, and dictation is one run of text. (7) **A page that cannot record** (`navigator.mediaDevices` missing, which is every `http://` address except localhost, so a phone on the network) no longer shows a dead microphone: the button stays enabled and pressing it shows one sentence saying the page needs https or localhost. No https is built here. (8) **Four proofs** that do not rest on anyone's say-so, run on the maintainer's computer and not in CI: 7a the word error rate, the median and 95th percentile time and the ten worst lines on the Italian FLEURS test split, through the product's own adapter; 7b the same clips as webm/opus and mp4/aac, each within two WER points of the WAV; 7c 5 s and 30 s of digital silence and of low room noise, which fail on any text; 7d the whole path in Chrome with a fake microphone. How to run them is in `openbot/docs/configuration.md`, "Local Whisper" | An app for people who are not developers cannot start with "get an API key". A local model is the only way to promise that a recording never leaves the machine, and whisper.cpp is the smallest runtime that serves the OpenAI path, so the adapter, the route, the limits and the composer did not have to change. The model is pinned by hash because a download over the network is the one thing in this design that runs unreviewed bytes; the tag is pinned by commit for the same reason. The flag is off because a model of 574 MB and a container are not a thing to install by surprise, and a person who set up their own service keeps it. Language and prompt are the two things that most change Whisper's answer on short Italian phrases, and both were free to add because the adapter already builds a form. Proofs live in scripts that the maintainer can read and run because the claim "it works in Italian on a phone-quality recording" needs numbers from the real model, and neither the author of the change nor the coordinator can supply them |

Limits:

- **CPU only in v0.1.** The image is built for the computer that builds it and uses no GPU. How long a request takes is what 7a reports on the maintainer's computer; it is not guessed here.
- **A recording is at most 120 seconds**, as for every provider, and one transcription per person at a time. A long dictation on a slow computer can pass the 60 seconds the route allows a provider and then fails with the existing "timed out" message.
- **A phone needs https.** On `http://<computer>:3010` the browser offers no microphone. The two ways out (use the computer itself, or put the app behind https) are in the configuration guide; neither is built.
- **The whisper port has no sign-in.** It is on loopback only, but anything running on that computer can reach it, and whisper-server answers any origin (`Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`, `server.cpp:739`). What a caller can do: send it audio and read the transcript (that costs CPU time and exposes nothing the caller did not already hold), and `POST /load` (`server.cpp:1175-1210`) with the path of a file in the container, which makes the server drop its model and, when the file is not a model, `exit(1)`, so the service stays down until `start.sh` is run again. Nothing is read back from the container's files. A web page in the person's browser needs to get past the browser's own protection of local addresses to do any of this. It stays as it is until a request is made to put the service anywhere but loopback, and that should come with a token. Whisper.cpp v1.9.5 has no option to turn `/load` off.
- **Not run here.** The image was not built and no model was downloaded in the environment that wrote this: Docker, Hugging Face and the Debian mirrors were not reachable. The Dockerfile's `--help` and `ffmpeg -version` smoke lines fail the build if the binary or ffmpeg is missing; everything else about the image is proved on the maintainer's computer by 7a to 7d. The model checksums were read from the repository's public Git LFS pointers (and, a second time, its file pages) because the files themselves could not be downloaded.
- **Hardening flags that were not exercised:** `read_only`, `cap_drop: ALL` and `no-new-privileges` on the service. They are standard Compose options and `docker compose config` accepts them; whether whisper-server runs under them is part of what 7a to 7d show.
- **Numbers are slightly pessimistic.** The word error rate does not rewrite numbers ("11" against "undici" is an error), and 7b has only five clips, so two points are two words in a hundred. It catches a broken format path, not a small loss.
- **FLEURS is read speech.** It says how the model does on clear Italian read aloud, not on a person talking in a kitchen. 7c and 7d are what say whether the product behaves on silence and on the real browser path.
- **Language detection** on a very short phrase can pick the wrong language when `TRANSCRIPTION_LANGUAGE` is unset.

### D25 amendment, 2026-11-11: measured on the maintainer's computer

First real run (32 threads, 61 GB, `large-v3-turbo-q5_0`, no language sent): word error rate **2.9%** on 100 FLEURS clips, no failed request, but **17.26 s** median and 20.85 s at the 95th percentile per request, and 7c failed ("Thank you." on digital silence, "." on room noise). The service log said `n_threads = 4 / 32`. What changed, from the source at v1.9.5:

- **Threads.** The server takes `min(4, hardware threads)` (`examples/server/server.cpp:73`, `--threads` at :210, applied at :936). The service now takes `WHISPER_THREADS`, by default half of `nproc` clamped to 4..16 (`openbot/whisper/threads.sh`, tested). Whether 16 is the best number for a given computer is for the maintainer to measure with the override.
- **Decoding.** The server is already **greedy** unless `beam_size > 1` (default -1, :82; strategy chosen at :927; `best_of` 2 at :81 only matters on the temperature fallback). So beam search was not the cause of the slowness and stays off; `WHISPER_BEAM_SIZE` (`--beam-size`, :218) lets the maintainer measure it without a rebuild. Temperature fallback (`temperature_inc` 0.2, :89) can only be switched off by a request field (:582): the server's `--no-fallback` is parsed (:228) and never used. It is not touched, because turning it off is a quality trade nobody has measured.
- **Silence, (a) options.** The server and library offer `--suppress-nst`, `--no-speech-thold` (:247) and `--logprob-thold` (:222), but a segment is dropped only when both the no-speech and the log-probability conditions hold (`src/whisper.cpp:7780`), and `logprob_thold` also drives the fallback re-decoding, so raising it is not a clean silence control. Not used.
- **Silence, (b) the response (tried, replaced).** `response_format=verbose_json` gives each segment `no_speech_prob` (`server.cpp:1154`). It was built and measured, and the service reports 0.000 on every segment, silence included, so a filter on it cannot work. Removed; see the second amendment.
- **7b** uses the ffmpeg in the built whisper image through `docker run` (no network, no capabilities, the caller's user, one folder mounted) when the host has none.

### D25 second amendment, 2026-11-11

Measured with 3348a56 (16 threads): 100 clips, language detected: `large-v3-turbo-q5_0` WER 2.8%, median 9.78 s, p95 17.42 s, 783 MB; `large-v3-turbo` 2.9%, 8.01 s, 8.79 s, 1.75 GB; `small` 7.9%, 1.83 s. Threads (q5_0, 30 clips): 8 gave 14.14 s, 16 about 9.8 s, 32 10.92 s, so the cap of 16 stays. At 4 threads, detection 17.26 s against 8.64 s with `it`, same 2.9%. 7b passed (wav, webm/opus and mp4/aac all 2.1%, through the image's ffmpeg). 7c failed again, with `no_speech_prob` 0.000 everywhere.

- **VAD on for the local service.** Hand-run by the maintainer on the same image, `--language it`: without VAD, 5 s and 30 s of silence gave "Grazie a tutti.", noise "Grazie."; with `--vad --vad-model ggml-silero-v5.1.2.bin` all four were empty, 2 s of speech kept, a full clip and a clip followed by 10 s of silence kept, WER on 30 clips 1.1% against 1.4% without, median 10.3 s against 8.7 s. The entrypoint passes `--vad --vad-model` (`server.cpp:260-261`, applied at :968-976) unless `WHISPER_VAD=off`. The VAD options stay at the server's defaults, none exposed: threshold 0.5 (:125), minimum speech 250 ms (:126), minimum silence 100 ms (:127), no maximum speech (:128), padding 30 ms (:129), overlap 0.1 (:130). The server's own default is VAD off (:123).
- **The VAD model is pinned like the Whisper ones**: `ggml-silero-v5.1.2.bin`, 885098 bytes, sha256 `29940d98d42b91fbd05ce489f3ecf7c72f0a42f027e4875919a28fb4c04ea2cf`, from https://huggingface.co/ggml-org/whisper-vad, read from the file's Git LFS pointer (`/raw/main/ggml-silero-v5.1.2.bin`) on 2026-11-11 and equal to what the maintainer's download gave. Fetched with the same checksum-verified code (`openbot/whisper/fetch-model.ts --vad`). Its licence is not recorded in NOTICE.md yet: the Silero VAD project is MIT, to be confirmed against the repository's model card.
- **A caution:** VAD together with a vocabulary prompt on dictation with long pauses can cut speech. Not reproduced here (the pause case below is the guard), which is why `WHISPER_VAD=off` exists and the prompt is optional.
- **The no-speech filter and the verbose request are gone.** The local adapter is back to the plain request; `joinSegments` stays because VAD output still has line breaks between segments.
- **7c guards the other way, with whole clips.** Besides the four silent cases: 2 s of speech then 10 s of silence still returns words of the sentence. The 2 s start at the speech onset (the first 20 ms window whose RMS is above 5% of the clip's own peak), 100 ms before it, and at least 1 s of them must be above that level, else the next cached clip is used: the first 2 s of a FLEURS clip are mostly lead-in silence, which VAD rightly drops. One more row, "a short word: 0.6 s from the onset, then 3 s of silence", is measured only: printed, never failing; one clip with a 3 s pause inserted in its middle is no more than 2 WER points worse than the clip alone; two whole clips with 5 s of silence between them and 3 s after return at least half of the words of each sentence. A clip cut mid-word proves nothing, so none is used.
- **Language is told, not detected.** Order: `TRANSCRIPTION_LANGUAGE`; else the browser's primary language subtag sent with the recording (the app has no language setting of its own: no i18n, no per-person preference), accepted only if it is exactly one of Whisper's two-letter codes, else dropped; else detection. Local service only: other providers ignore the hint, so their request is unchanged. Limit: a person whose browser is not in the language they dictate must set `TRANSCRIPTION_LANGUAGE`.
- Default model unchanged (`large-v3-turbo-q5_0`); the measured table is in `configuration.md` for the maintainer to choose from.

## 9. The person's language when nobody wrote (D26)

Added on 2026-11-12. On his own stack the maintainer, who reads Italian only, got a check-in by Iris ("Checked your mail and calendar ... Outcome: done"), its suggestion card ("Decide whether to keep the Google account ...") and the morning brief ("Morning.", "Needs you", "Can wait", "Couldn't check") in English, while every answer to a message he typed in Italian was Italian. Every member's shared rules start "Reply in the language the person writes in." In a turn nobody wrote, that gives no language.

| # | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| D26 | **One optional deployment setting, `FREEDUET_PERSON_LANGUAGE` (a two-letter code), tells the members and the server which language the person reads.** (1) **Validated at start** against the list `TRANSCRIPTION_LANGUAGE` uses (`server/src/dictation/languages.ts`); anything else stops the server with a sentence naming the setting (`loadConfig`, `server/src/freeduet/person-language.ts`). (2) **The member is told in its runtime notes.** OpenBot adds `openbotPersonLanguage: { code, name }` to the run's `forwardedProps`, the place that already carries `openbotBotId`, `openbotDeploymentTools` and `openbotRun` (`server/src/copilot.ts`, `runWith`, through `withPersonLanguage`); a value a caller put under that key is dropped. agent-freeduet reads it tolerantly (`agui.py`; a value that is not a two-letter lowercase code with a short plain name is ignored) and `person_language.py` adds one note to the run's notes, which `segment.py` hands to the turn and to the report guard's follow-up: the language, "when a turn starts without a message from the person (a check-in, a routine, the morning brief, a report that comes back from a colleague), write everything the person will read in that language, cards and their titles included", and "when the person writes in another language, answer in theirs". Unset, no key is sent, no note is added, and the system text and the prompt are byte-identical to before. (3) **The shared rule in all ten prompts** gains one sentence after "Reply in the language the person writes in.": "When a turn starts without a message from the person (a check-in, a routine, the morning brief), write in the person's language: the one your runtime note names, else the language of their latest messages in this conversation." With no setting the fallback is the language of the person's last messages, which is what a member would have done anyway. (4) **The fixed sentences the server writes in those turns have an Italian form**, selected by the same setting, in one table beside the helper (`personText`, English by default): see the list below. (5) **The model that sorts the brief's mail** (`openduet/triage.ts`) is told "Write the reason in <language>." only when the setting is present. | The members' rule has to name a language for a turn the person did not write, and the one place that knows it is the deployment's own settings. The channel is the one OpenBot already uses for per-run facts to agent-freeduet, so there is no second one, and nothing to configure on agent-freeduet's own container. A table of two languages is the smallest thing that fixes the maintainer's problem without starting an i18n framework. |

**What was code and what was model text** (found by reading the turns' sources):

| Where it appears | Written by | This change |
|---|---|---|
| The check-in's frame (`shared/check-in.ts`), the research instruction (`proactive/engine.ts`, `researchInstruction`), the routine's frame (`shared/routine-firing.ts`), the morning brief's frame (`shared/morning-brief.ts`), the first day's frame (`shared/first-day.ts`) | Code, an instruction to the model that the person never reads (the app draws a line of its own, or nothing, in its place) | Left in English |
| Check-in text ("Checked your mail and calendar ... Outcome: done Done: ..."), a suggestion's title and why ("Decide whether to keep the Google account ...", "Google emailed at 14:23 UTC today ..."), a routine's report, the sentence after the brief's card | The member (model text) | The runtime note and the shared rule |
| The brief card's headings and rows: "Morning.", "Needs you", "Can wait", "Couldn't check", "N emails can wait", "All day", "Show N more" | The app (`app/src/components/duet/morning-brief-card.tsx`, `app/src/lib/copilot/morning-brief-tool.tsx`) | Translated in the amendment below (Italian; English for any other language) |
| The brief's reason for mail it sorted by rule (four sentences, `triage.ts`), "Could not be sorted, so it is here to be safe.", the brief's gap lines ("Only the newest N of about M ...", "N of M messages could not be sorted ...") and the label "Sorting" (`brief.ts`) | The server, fixed | Italian form |
| The "From <app>" line under a suggestion in a push, a Slack line or a text (`suggestionNotice`) | The server, fixed | Italian form |
| The reason on each brief row for mail the rules did not settle | The model that sorts mail (`triage.ts`), not a member | Told the language when the setting is present |
| A routine's "This routine failed: ..." and "switched off" lines (`routines/runner.ts`), the `Source:` label in a started suggestion's ask (`shared/task-brief.ts` reads it back), the plugin store's refusal sentences that a brief's gap line repeats, conversation titles | The server, fixed or from another model call | Left in English |

Limits:

- **One language for the whole deployment.** FreeDuet is self-hosted, usually for one person, and the app has no per-person language. A deployment shared by people who read different languages needs a per-person setting, which this is not.
- **A person can choose their own language from chat (PR #222, 15 November 2026).** One language per person, kept in `users.preferences.freeduetLanguage`, set only with a card in the person's own live turn (`server/src/freeduet/person-language-choice.ts`, `docs/design/settings-in-chat.md` 8.10.3). It changes the language note in a member's run and nothing else: the app's cards and the server's fixed sentences still follow `FREEDUET_PERSON_LANGUAGE`. A person who never chose gets the same run as before. No eval has run on it.
- **The fixed sentences exist in English and Italian only.** A setting of `de` makes the members write German and leaves the server's own sentences in English.
- **The app's own interface stays English, except the two cards and the line above a check-in** (the amendment below). "Message Iris", the menus, the settings, the sheets, the tool lines and the rest are drawn by the app, which has no i18n. A grep finds about 280 literal JSX text nodes and about 250 `label`, `title`, `text` or `message` properties in roughly 300 component files: on the order of 500 strings, not an inventory. Translating them is a separate, later job (a locale the app reads, and a message table), and the maintainer should decide it.
- **A model can still answer in the wrong language.** The note and the rule are instructions. Whether the members follow them on a live check-in is for the maintainer's stack to show; no live run was made here.
- **Members that run inside the server (`FREEDUET_ROUTER=off`, built-in members, D2) are not told the language**: the note is agent-freeduet's. They get the shared rule's fallback, the language of the person's latest messages. The server's own sentences and the brief's reasons follow the setting in both modes.
- The brief's rule reasons and gap lines are written once per brief; a brief already in a thread keeps the language it was made in.

### Amendment (2026-11-12): the brief card, the suggestion card and the check-in line in the person's language

The maintainer asked: "translate the cards for the selected language". D26 made the members and the server's own sentences follow `FREEDUET_PERSON_LANGUAGE`; the cards the person opens every day still had English written in the app. **Three things are now translated, and nothing else in the app is** (apart from the four things that belong to them, in the second amendment below). This is not the interface translation the limits above leave for later.

**How the app learns the language.** From the server, not from the browser. `/api/capabilities` carries `personLanguage` (`server/src/app.ts`) when the setting is present: the code `personLanguageFrom` already validated against the dictation list, so the only value that can be served is a known two-letter code. Unset, the key is absent and the answer is what it always was (a test pins the key list). The app reads it with the query it already runs for the other deployment facts (`deploymentCapabilitiesQueryOptions`, `staleTime: Infinity`; it keeps a two-letter lowercase code and drops anything else), and `PersonLanguageProvider` (`app/src/freeduet/person-language/use-card-text.tsx`, mounted in `routes/_authed.tsx`) hands it to the cards through a React context. Why this channel and not the other: `scripts/generate-app-config.ts` writes `app/src/lib/generated/application-config.ts` at build time, so a language there would need a rebuild of the app after every change to `.env`; `/api/capabilities` is built from `config` when the server starts, so editing `.env` and restarting the server is all it takes, as for every other setting in this section. The browser's own language is not used: the person who reads Italian may use a browser, or a borrowed one, in English, and the members already write in the setting's language.

**What is translated** (`app/src/freeduet/person-language/cards.ts`, one table per language, English first, no i18n library):

| Where | Text |
|---|---|
| Brief card (`morning-brief-card.tsx`, `morning-brief-tool.tsx`) | the region's name, the date line, the greeting by hour (with and without a name), "Needs you", "Can wait", "Today", "Couldn't check", the empty-brief sentence, "N emails can wait" and "A, B, C and N more", "All day" and the clock times, "Show N more" and its screen-reader name, "Start", its screen-reader name and "Started", "Couldn't make the brief", and the words Start sends into the thread as the person's own (`Take care of X's email: ...`), so the answer comes back in the person's language |
| Suggestion card (`suggestion-card.tsx`, `chat-suggestion.tsx`, `offer-task-tool.tsx`) | "<Bot> could take this on", "Ready to start", "From <app>", "Hide", "Start", "Started", "Hidden", "Started. <Bot> is on it.", and the two words under the card while it is dragged (`SwipeToAnswer` gained an optional `labels`; Tasks, the other user, does not pass it and is unchanged) |
| The line above a check-in (`chat-transcript.tsx`, `CheckInLine`) | "Checked your mail and calendar" and its time |

**What is not translated.** The rest of the interface (see the limit above). Within these cards: every word the mail, the member or the server wrote (a row's title and reason, a gap's reason, a suggestion's title and why), which are the member's text and the server's sentences, already covered above. The four leftovers this paragraph used to list (the running lines, the notification, the onboarding demo, the first paint) are done: see the second amendment below.

**The fallback.** A language with no table here draws today's English, with the browser's own locale for dates and times, exactly as before: for `en`, for no setting at all, for `de` (the members write German, the cards stay English) and for a server too old to send the field. Italian is the one table shipped; another language is one more object of the same shape. `it` is typed by the shape of the English table, so a string cannot be added to English without its Italian form, an Italian key English lacks is refused, and so is a plural written as a plain string (`tsc`, pinned by a test with `@ts-expect-error`). Plurals use `Intl.PluralRules` of the language ("1 email può aspettare", "3 email possono aspettare"), dates and times `Intl.DateTimeFormat` of the language ("giovedì 22 ottobre", "15:00"). One more limit, as it stood for the first amendment: a card on screen when the first answer from `/api/capabilities` arrives was drawn in English for that moment and redrawn. The second amendment below closes it for every visit after the first.

### Second amendment (2026-11-12): the four leftovers of the cards

The maintainer does not read English and asked for the four things the first amendment left in English. Same rule as before: with the language unset, `en` or a language with no table, the output is today's, byte for byte (pinned by `app/tests/freeduet-card-language-rest.test.tsx`, each case also run against the English of today).

| Leftover | Now | How it reads the language |
|---|---|---|
| The running lines of the two FreeDuet tools, and the pill under the header's orb | "Leggo posta e calendario" / "Ho letto posta e calendario" (`morning_brief`), "Aggiungo un'attività" / "Ho aggiunto un'attività" (`offer_task`). Only these two tools: the rest of the shared vocabulary in `lib/plugins/tool-name.ts` (mail, web, memory, the other own tools, "Thinking it through") is the interface translation, still to do | `ownToolLabel`, `readToolName`, `stepPhrase` and `personToolLabel` take an optional last argument, the language. `ownToolLabel` asks `ownToolLineIn` (`person-language/tool-lines.ts`) first and falls back to the shared English. The tool's line (`BriefToolView`, `AddingTaskLine`, `ServerToolLine`) gets it from `useCardLanguage()`, the pill from `stepFor(..., language)` called in `ConversationView`. Both read the same function, so they cannot say different things; a test draws both beside each other |
| The browser notification of a check-in | "<task> e un'altra" / "<task> e altre N", the plural by `Intl.PluralRules` like the rest of the table. The task's title is the member's text and is left alone | `useCheckInNotifications` reads `useCardLanguage()` where it runs (under `_authed`, in `OngoingChat`) and keeps it in a ref, as it already did for the Bot. No second request: the context is fed by the capabilities query the app already runs. `checkInNotice` takes the language as an optional third argument |
| The onboarding demo | The brief demo and the search demo are Italian from the caption above the card to the rows in it, the date line and the screen-reader label. The approval and task demos draw cards the table does not translate and stay English whole | The onboarding route lives in `routes/_authed/onboarding.tsx`, so it is under `PersonLanguageProvider` and reads the language like any card (`useCardLanguage`, `useCardText`). A route outside `_authed` would have no provider and show English, which is what a card does without one |
| The first paint | The language the server last gave is kept in `localStorage` (`freeduet.person-language`, the two-letter code and nothing else), and the first paint uses it while `/api/capabilities` is out. The server's answer always wins: it replaces the kept code, or removes it when the server sends no language | `person-language/stored-language.ts`: read with the same `/^[a-z]{2}$/` check as the query, every read and write inside `try/catch`, so a browser that blocks storage draws as before. A request that fails is not an answer: the kept code stays, and with none kept the cards are English |

Limits that remain. The very first visit of a browser has nothing kept, so its first paint is still English for the time of one request (and the onboarding demo, shown to a new person, is the likeliest place to see it). A folded group of tool steps in the transcript (`tool-activity.tsx`, drawn only for a tool the app has no renderer for) still names its steps in English: its sentence ("Searched your email and read 2 pages") is joined in English, and these two tools have a renderer, so they never fold. The Bot's access page lists tools with `readToolName` and passes no language, so it stays English with the rest of that page.

## 10. A new email goes out on the person's own written request (D27)

Added on 2026-11-12, by the maintainer's explicit decision while D24's fifth tool was being built: when he writes "write to <address>", it must do it. He has already approved by writing it, and a second card asking the same thing is noise. The decision covers `gmail_send_message` only. `gmail_send_reply` is exactly as it was, and nothing else about an account action changes.

| # | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| D27 | **A new email is sent without a card when, and only when, every one of these holds, checked on the server at the approval gate and never from what the model says about itself; in every other case it waits for the person.** (1) **The run is the person's own live chat turn.** The run carries no initiator, which is what a turn started from the app carries. A routine, a check-in, a memory run, the deployment, a task, a message delivered from another channel, a group conversation, the continuation after another approval and every hand-off carry an explicit initiator and fail this condition (`typed-recipients.ts`, `recipientsTypedByPerson`, called from `plugin-store/calls.ts`). (2) **The message is recent: the latest message the person typed in that conversation, the one that started the turn, and no other (a window of one).** An earlier message cannot be told from a routine's stored prompt or a check-in's text that now sits in the same history, so none is trusted. (3) **Every recipient is one whole word of that message**, equal ignoring case and surrounding spaces. A message that is not plain text (attachments are a list of parts), is longer than 600 characters (a paste, the commonest way for somebody else's words to reach a message the person sent), is a hand-off's envelope (`handoff-` id), or has another role (a Bot's text, a tool result, mail, a page, memory) is never looked at. A recipient that only appears inside a longer address, on another domain, with an added dot or a look-alike letter, or that the member corrected or completed ("x@gmail.co" typed, "x@gmail.com" sent), is a different string and asks. A recipient beyond those typed asks. **Subject and body are the member's own words and are not matched;** the caps and the validation of D24's amendment still apply. **Everything else still asks**, with the whole email on the card: an address from an email, a page or a tool result, a corrected or completed address, an extra recipient, a routine, a check-in, a hand-off, a turn the server started, an address typed in an earlier message, and a deployment with no way to ask (it refuses, nothing is sent). **After a send without a card the member says plainly that it was sent, to whom, its subject and the text** (Otto's prompt and skill). **One send per request:** the same email (person, run, recipients, subject, body) is tried once per run, a failure included, because a half-delivered send must not go to the others twice; a repeat is refused, and the person is told so in words (D37). The audit row for such a send says `approvedBy: person_request`. The person's own "ask before making changes" setting and any team rule that asks still open a card as for any write | "The person's own written request is the approval." Asking again what the person has just written is the kind of friction a personal assistant should not have, and the person already sees, in their own words, who it goes to. What the rule never does is take the Bot's word for it: the server reads the conversation it holds, and the only thing that can stand in for the card is something a person typed to start this very turn. Delegation must never launder an account action, so a Bot writing an address into a brief, a memory or a stored prompt is not "typed by the person", and any doubt resolves to asking |

### Amendment (2026-11-12): the person asks Iris, and Iris hands the email to Otto (D27a)

The maintainer talks to Iris, not to Otto, so "send an email to ana@example.com saying good morning" always reached Otto as a hand-off and always cost a card in a scratch thread he never sees. D27a lets it go out with no card **when the server itself can show that the hop came from the person's own typed message, and only then.** Nothing the model writes can set, change or read what the server relies on.

| # | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| D27a | **The mark.** When a member calls `message_bot` during the person's own live chat turn (the run's continuation carries no initiator, exactly D27 condition 1), the server records on the hop's own queue row, beside the envelope and never inside it, who the person is, which conversation and run the hop was made in, and the id and a SHA-256 of the person's latest typed message of that turn (`agents/handoff-request.ts`, `handedRequestOf`, called from `agents/handoff-tool.ts`). It is computed from the signed run assertion and the run's own input messages. The tool's arguments, the brief and the constraints are not parameters of it, and a `request` key a model puts in them is ignored. A hop made from inside a hop, from a routine, a check-in, a memory run, the deployment, a task, a delivered message or a group conversation gets no mark; neither does a relay or a notice, which are built elsewhere with no origin. **The load.** At the approval gate, when the member who received the hop calls `gmail_send_message`, the server verifies the run assertion it signed (the member, the person, one hop deep), finds the hop's row by the claim the assertion names, checks the row is still leased to this run, addressed to this member and this person and not a relay, checks the mark names this person, this hop's conversation and this hop's run, and then reads the ORIGIN conversation itself and takes the person's latest message there. That message must still be the one the mark names (same id, same text hash). It is then given to the same pure rule as D27, `recipientsTypedByPerson`: one message, a plain string, at most 600 characters, every recipient a whole word of it ignoring case, one to five plain addresses. The receiver's own history is never consulted. **The limits, each held by a test through the real gate:** one hop only; the mark is good only for the hop made in that turn and only while that turn is still the person's latest message in the origin conversation (a newer message, or a hop left over from an earlier turn, asks); the same person on both sides; a brief that names an address the person did not type asks; a member that changes the address asks; an extra recipient asks; a call that claims to be the person's own still asks; a conversation that cannot be read asks; **one send per person's message:** the same email (person, origin message, recipients, subject, body) goes out once however many hops were made from it, a failure included, and a repeat is refused, and the person is told so in words (D37). The audit row says `approvedBy: person_request`. The person's own "ask before making changes" setting and any team rule that asks still open a card | The person's own written request is the approval, and it does not stop being theirs because they wrote it to the head of the team. What would be wrong is trusting the hop's text, so the server decides from three things only it holds: its own signed assertion, its own queue row, and the conversation it stores. Any doubt resolves to asking |

**After a send with no card, the person is told.** Otto's prompt already says to tell them that it was sent, to whom, its subject and the text. Iris's "How you handle results" now says the same when a colleague reports an email sent, and that her three-sentence chat limit does not cut it short, so the answer the person reads from Iris says it plainly.

**What is not verified, and what would change it.**

- **Not run end to end on a live stack.** The conditions are proved by tests through the real gate, the real queue rows and the real thread store (`send-message-approval.integration.test.ts`, `handoff-request.integration.test.ts`, `handoff-request.test.ts`), with a fake mail backend. No eval was added: the eval stack has no Gmail. Whether the person's message is already readable in the origin conversation while Iris's turn is still running depends on the thread store reading the running turn (`threads/store.ts`, `getThreadMessages`, which includes the live run); a deployment where it is not readable fails to the card.
- **A built-in (in-process) Bot** gets no hand-off claim on its run assertion, so a hop to it asks. Only the remote members that FreeDuet runs through agent-freeduet are covered.
- **A shared channel's other members.** The conversation holds messages by several people in a channel; the origin message is whatever the conversation stores as the person's latest, the same limit the direct rule has.
- **"Once per message" is in the server's process** (like "once per run"): a restart forgets it.
- **The forward hop is a scratch thread.** When a hop does ask, its card is in the person's approval list and not in a conversation they are looking at; that is as before.
- **Subject and body are the member's**, as in D27: a hand-off brief can change what the email says, not who gets it.

**What is not covered, and what would be needed.**

- **A hand-off asks unless the server itself recorded where it came from.** The rule above is the direct case. A hand-off carries an explicit initiator and fails the direct rule, and the receiver's history holds a Bot's envelope, so the person's message cannot be read from it. The amendment below closes that case for one hop and nothing else; every hand-off that does not meet every condition of the amendment still asks.
- **The window is one message.** An address typed two messages ago asks again. A wider window needs the same provenance mark on each message, because role `user` also holds the server's own messages (a routine's prompt, a task's, a delivered one) with nothing to tell them apart in an old history.
- **What the person's words cannot show.** A message the person sent by tapping a suggestion a Bot wrote is still theirs, and an address inside a short paste in their own message counts. The card exists for every other case.
- **Subject and body are the member's.** A prompt injected through mail the member read in the same turn can change what the email says, not who gets it. Several different emails to the same typed address in one run are each sent (only an exact repeat is stopped); a per-run cap is a decision for the maintainer.
- **The "once per run" memory is in the server's process.** A restart forgets it, and a new run is a new request.
- Evals: the cases that say "any email goes through the send tool and its approval" (`iris-4`, `portia-3`) are about a member without the tool, or an address nobody typed, and still hold; no eval for a typed address was added, because the eval stack has no Gmail connected.

### Amendment (2026-11-15): a message from the microphone, or from a remote session, is not the person's typed turn

Two marks narrow D27 and D27a, and Full access with them. Neither widens anything.

- **Voice (PR #217).** A message whose id starts with `voice-` (dictation sent with one press, the spoken-message sheet's Send, anything a voice call's model writes) never earns the shortcut and never counts as the person's own written turn for Full access: it asks with the card, like a hand-off. The app sets the mark; nothing a Bot, a tool, a page or a mail writes can set it. The server cannot know the origin by itself, so a client that leaves the mark off is the person's own browser, inside D27's trust boundary. Text placed in the box and sent with the Send button counts as typed. One `if` each in `typed-recipients.ts`, `handedRequestOf` and `full-access.ts`; `approvals/policy.ts` is untouched.
- **Remote (PR #221).** A chat turn from a signed-in session that does not hold the installation's cookie carries the server-set initiator `remote`, and gets the same answer: the card (section 11).

Not run live: no real microphone, voice call, phone or provider.

## 11. A secret per installation, so a container is not taken for the person (D28)

Added on 2026-11-14, the follow-up that #116 left open (the roadmap item "A secret per installation for the no-sign-in mode"). With no sign-in (`OPENBOT_SINGLE_USER`) the person is the administrator, and since #116 that is only a request whose TCP peer is this machine's loopback. That settles Linux and WSL, where a container reaches the server on the Docker bridge and its peer is the bridge. It does not settle Docker Desktop (Windows, macOS), where a container that calls the host's published port is carried through the VM's own proxy and arrives as 127.0.0.1, the same peer as the person's browser. A Bot's computer is such a container, and it runs what a member, or a page it opened, asks of it.

| # | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| D28 | **In single-user mode, a request from this machine's loopback is the person only when it also holds this installation's secret.** (1) **One secret, made once, by the script that already keeps the install's other secrets.** `scripts/start.sh` writes `FREEDUET_INSTALL_SECRET` (32 random bytes as hex) to the settings file, mode 600, the first time it runs without one. An install from before this gets it on its first start after the update, and that start restarts the server, which reads its settings once. The script never prints it and never exports it: Compose and the Bots' containers are children of the script.
